❓ Question

Which SLURM partition on FASRC Cannon should I submit GPU jobs to, and what flag do I use to request GPUs?

🎯 Retrieval Check

Expected Document(s): https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs

Retrieved Documents: docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/gpgpu-computing-on-the-cluster, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/vscode-remote-development-via-ssh-or-tunnel, slurm.schedmd.com/gres.html, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/kempner-partitions, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/fasse, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Notes/mmpose.md, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/vscode-remote-development-via-ssh-or-tunnel, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/AI/AITools/ollama.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Notes/alphapose.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Notes/amber.md

✅ Status: FULLY CORRECT

🤖 archi's Answer
Based on the FASRC documentation, here's what you need to know for submitting GPU jobs on the FASRC cluster (Cannon): ### GPU Partition Use the **`gpu_test`** partition for testing and experimentation with GPU resources [Running Jobs – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs/). ### GPU Request Flag Include the following flag in your SLURM submission script: ```bash #SBATCH --gres=gpu:n ``` Where `n` is the number of GPUs you need (1–8) [Running Jobs – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs/). ### Example Submission Script ```bash #!/bin/bash #SBATCH --partition gpu_test #SBATCH --gres=gpu:1 #SBATCH --time=0-12:00:00 #SBATCH --mem=64GB #SBATCH --cpus-per-task=8 # Your GPU job commands here ``` ### Important Notes - The `gpu_test` partition is intended for **interactive, testing, and experimentation only** [Running Jobs – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs/). - These nodes have 64 cores and are equipped with 4× Nvidia A100 GPUs in Multi-Instance GPU (MIG) mode [Running Jobs – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs/). - Maximum of 2 jobs allowed in this partition, with a 12-hour run time limit [Running Jobs – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs/). - Users must request less than 8 CPUs and 64GB RAM per MIG GPU [Running Jobs – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs/). - Multiple partition submissions to `gpu_test` are forbidden [Running Jobs – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs/). For more details, see the [GPU Computing on the FASRC cluster](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/gpgpu-computing-on-the-cluster/) guide.
✅ Expected Answer
Use the gpu partition (or gpu_test for short test jobs). Request GPUs with --gres=gpu:N in your SLURM submit script, e.g. --gres=gpu:1.

  • https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs

Question 107: question_107

❓ Question

Where on the FASRC Cannon filesystem should I store large datasets that I don't need backed up?

🎯 Retrieval Check

Expected Document(s): https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/cluster-storage

Retrieved Documents: docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/quickstart-guide, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/fasrc-cluster-storage-policy, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/policy-scratch, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/getting-started-with-fasrc-storage, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/iqss-cannon-quickstart-guide

❌ Status: INCORRECT

🤖 archi's Answer
For large datasets that you **don't need backed up**, you should use the **scratch filesystem** at `/n/netscratch` or the **holylabs** directory at `/n/holylabs`. * **`/n/netscratch`**: This is a large, shared temporary scratch filesystem (50TB per lab) designed for high I/O jobs. It is **not backed up**, and files older than 90 days will be automatically deleted [Scratch – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/policy-scratch). * **`/n/holylabs`**: This is a lab directory (4TB) that also has **no backup** [Getting Started with FASRC Storage – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/getting-started-with-fasrc-storage). Both options are complimentary and suitable for transient or large-scale research data that does not require backup. If you need longer-term retention without backup, `/n/holylabs` may be preferable since it doesn't have the 90-day purge policy that `/n/netscratch` does [Getting Started with FASRC Storage – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/getting-started-with-fasrc-storage).
✅ Expected Answer
Use /n/holyscratch01// for fast scratch storage. It is not backed up and is periodically purged of older files.
🎯 Expected Source Documents
  • https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/cluster-storage

Question 108: question_108

❓ Question

My SLURM job on Cannon was killed with an out-of-memory error. How do I diagnose what the actual memory peak was, and how do I request more memory for the next submission?

🎯 Retrieval Check

Expected Document(s): https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs

Retrieved Documents: docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/faq, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/slurm-memory-limits, slurm.schedmd.com/slurm.conf.html, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, slurm.schedmd.com/faq.html

✅ Status: FULLY CORRECT

🤖 archi's Answer
Here's how to diagnose the memory peak and request more memory for your next job on Cannon: ### 1. Diagnose the actual memory peak The memory value SLURM records for a job killed by `OUT_OF_MEMORY` is **inaccurate**. To get a true measurement, you need a job that **completes successfully**. * **For a completed (successful) job:** Use the `jobstats` command to see the memory usage: ```bash jobstats JOBID ``` This will show you the actual memory peak used by the job. Set your next `--mem` request to a value slightly higher than what `jobstats` reports, as `--mem` is a hard upper limit. * **For a running job:** You can also run `jobstats JOBID` to monitor its current memory usage in real-time. * **For historical summaries:** You can use the `seff-account` tool to get summary information about jobs over a period of time. ### 2. Request more memory for your next submission Add the `--mem` flag to your `#SBATCH` directives in your job script: ```bash #SBATCH --mem 4G ``` * `X` is the maximum amount of memory your job will use **per node**, in MB. * You can also use the `G` suffix for GB (e.g., `--mem 4G` for 4 GB). **Tip:** Start with a large request (e.g., `--mem 4G` or higher) to ensure your job runs, then use `jobstats` on a successful run to find the actual peak. You can then reduce the request to the smallest amount needed, which helps the scheduler find a node more easily. Note that job slots on Cannon average about 4000 MB per core. For more details, see the [Slurm memory limits](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/slurm-memory-limits/) guide.
✅ Expected Answer
After the job ends, run sacct -j --format=JobID,MaxRSS,ReqMem to see the peak memory the job used (MaxRSS) versus what was requested (ReqMem). In your next submit script, increase the memory request via --mem=G (whole-job) or --mem-per-cpu=G (per-task), aiming a bit above the observed MaxRSS so future variations don't kill the job again.
🎯 Expected Source Documents
  • https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs

Question 109: question_109

❓ Question

I want to run a multi-node MPI program on Cannon that uses one GPU per node. What does the SLURM submit script need to specify, and how do I launch the MPI ranks?

🎯 Retrieval Check

Expected Document(s): https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/mpi-message-passing-interface

Retrieved Documents: github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Training/Distributed_GPU_Computing/Exercise6/run.sbatch, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/GPU_Computing/Dist_GPU_Computing/NCCL_and_CUDA/MultiNode/ncclAllGather_mpi.cu, slurm.schedmd.com/gres.conf.html, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/fasse

⚠️ Status: PARTIALLY CORRECT (1/2 sources found)

🤖 archi's Answer
To run a multi-node MPI program on Cannon with **one GPU per node**, you need to configure your SLURM submit script and launch command as follows: ### 1. SLURM Submit Script Directives The key directives for a multi-node, one-GPU-per-node setup are: * **`--nodes=`**: Specify the number of nodes (e.g., `--nodes=2`). * **`--ntasks-per-node=1`**: This is critical. It tells SLURM to launch one MPI rank per node. * **`--gpus-per-node=1`**: This allocates exactly one GPU for each node in the job. * **`--cpus-per-task=`**: Allocate enough CPU cores for your application (e.g., `--cpus-per-task=4`). * **`--partition=gpu`**: Use the GPU partition. **Example `#SBATCH` block:** ```bash #SBATCH --job-name=my_mpi_gpu #SBATCH --nodes=2 #SBATCH --ntasks-per-node=1 #SBATCH --gpus-per-node=1 #SBATCH --cpus-per-task=4 #SBATCH --time=01:00:00 #SBATCH --partition=gpu ``` ### 2. Launching MPI Ranks Use `srun` to launch your MPI executable. Since you have one task per node and one GPU per node, each rank will automatically be bound to one GPU. **Example launch command:** ```bash srun --ntasks=$SLURM_NTASKS --ntasks-per-node=1 \ --cpu-bind=cores \ mpirun -np $SLURM_NTASKS ./my_mpi_program ``` ### Key Points * **One GPU per Rank**: By setting `--ntasks-per-node=1` and `--gpus-per-node=1`, SLURM ensures each MPI rank gets exclusive access to one GPU. * **Environment Variables**: SLURM provides environment variables like `$SLURM_NODEID` and `$SLURM_LOCALID` that your program can use to identify which GPU to use (e.g., `cuda:0` for the first GPU on the node). * **NCCL/Communication**: If using NCCL for GPU communication, ensure your application is configured to use the correct network interface (often `ib0` or `eth0` on Cannon). For a more detailed example with distributed training, see the [FASRC User Codes repository](https://github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Training/Distributed_GPU_Computing/Exercise6/run.sbatch).
✅ Expected Answer
In your submit script: choose a GPU-enabled partition (e.g. gpu), set --nodes= with --ntasks-per-node=1 and --gres=gpu:1 (per-node GPU request), load the MPI and CUDA modules with module load. Launch the program with srun (not mpirun directly) so SLURM places one rank per node with the bound GPU; e.g. srun --mpi=pmix ./my_program. Use sinfo and scontrol show partition gpu to confirm node configuration before submitting.
🎯 Expected Source Documents
  • https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs
  • https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/mpi-message-passing-interface
